# planrun P6c — The TUI

**Goal:** `planrun tui [<plans-dir>]`, the TUI of `docs/tui-design.md` (§11's v1 scope): a new crate, `crates/planrun-tui`, over planrun-model, planrun-parse and planrun-exec, composed by planrun-cli. First the two decisions this phase adds (D93: the TUI is tested in-process; D94: how `planrun tui` starts and reads keys). Then the TUI, each task leaving a usable `planrun tui`: the shell (header, footer, update indicator, the size gate) with the Plans view; the plan view in Inspect; Run, with the runner on its worker thread, the journal observer and the dock with every `QuestionKind`; the `[o]`, `[v]` and `[d]` round trips and Ctrl-C; History, the resume preview and resume; the command palette and help; a dock in a small terminal. Last, the architecture, extending and CLAUDE.md lines and the crate's README with the manual check list. Nothing in planrun-parse or planrun-exec changes; planrun-cli gains the `tui` subcommand and the update check the TUI's indicator shows.

- **P6c-T1** (docs): D93 and D94 with their amended-by lines in D82 and D89; `docs/tui-design.md` §1, §6.A, §6.B and §11; the roadmap's P6c.
- **P6c-T2** (planrun-tui, planrun-cli): the crate (ratatui, unicode-width), the colour tokens, the glyphs, the cell-grid snapshots, the app, the driver and the terminal (the panic hook, interrupts once), background jobs that report a panic as their failure, the shell and the Plans view; `planrun tui` and its update check.
- **P6c-T3** (planrun-tui): the navigator, unified diffs, and the plan view in Inspect: ACTION, DRY RUN and the assumed policy (D84).
- **P6c-T4** (planrun-tui): Run: the runner on its worker thread behind a channel `Ui`, the journal observer, CHANGES and the console, and the dock with one layout per `QuestionKind` (D85, D86, D90).
- **P6c-T5** (planrun-tui): the `[o]` shell, `[v]` and `[d]` round trips, Ctrl-C with its confirm and `end_now` (D91), and `q` twice at a commit's confirmation.
- **P6c-T6** (planrun-tui): History (F4), the resume preview and resume from it (D85).
- **P6c-T7** (planrun-tui): the command palette (`ctrl+k`, `ctrl+p`) and help (F1, `?`).
- **P6c-T8** (planrun-tui): a dock that opens below 100 × 30 is drawn alone, the console's spinner turns, and two test modules move to `_tests.rs` files.
- **P6c-T9** (docs): architecture.md §2 and §7.1, extending.md §7, CLAUDE.md's layout, the crate's README with the manual check list, `docs/tui-design.md`'s status.

**Base:** `bba2053` (`Handover to continue work on TUI`); there fmt, clippy (with and without `e2e`), `cargo test` with `PLANRUN_REQUIRE_SHELLS=1` (27 `test result: ok` lines, 291 tests) and the `e2e` test pass. After P6c-T9: 35 lines, 323 tests. Execute from the commit of this plan. Gate per task: `cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo clippy -p planrun-cli --all-targets --features e2e -- -D warnings`, `cargo test`, `cargo test -p planrun-cli --features e2e --test run`, with `PLANRUN_REQUIRE_SHELLS=1` (D82).

**Shell and environment:** PowerShell 7 on Windows; Rust 1.95.0 (stable; ratatui 0.30.2 needs 1.88); git on `PATH`; pwsh 7 and Git for Windows (D63). P6c-T2's first build fetches the new crates from crates.io: ratatui 0.30.2 (with ratatui-core, ratatui-crossterm, ratatui-widgets) and crossterm 0.29.0 with what they bring; unicode-width 0.2.3 is in `Cargo.lock` already. Their D9 rows are D92's.

**Before P6c-T1:** this plan committed, and a clean `git status --porcelain`. To run the plan with planrun itself, run a copy outside `target/`, since every gate builds `target/debug/planrun.exe`: `cargo build --release`, `Copy-Item target/release/planrun.exe $env:TEMP\planrun-p6c.exe`, then `& $env:TEMP\planrun-p6c.exe run docs/plans/2026-11-27-p6c-tui.md --auto read,build,modify`. Every commit asks to confirm its prose expectation: **answer `y`, not `q`** (the P5→P6 handover's item 10, still open). The real plans stay in `tests/corpus/local/` (D16); this plan quotes none of them.

**How this plan was verified:** every task was built in a scratch clone of `bba2053` outside this repository (`core.autocrlf=false`), one commit per task. At each commit the gate passes, and so do the task's targeted commands; the summary lines below come from those runs. Also at each commit: `cargo clippy --all-targets --target x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu -- -D warnings`, for the Linux build CI runs. The plan was generated from that history (full contents from `git show`, exact diffs from `git diff`, blob ids from `git rev-parse`), `planrun check` passes on it, and it was replayed by planrun on a fresh clone with `core.autocrlf=true`: every commit step committed, `git status --porcelain` was empty after each, and the replay's final tree is the scratch tree.

## Review rulings

From the review of the first version of this plan, as decided by Rens; each is in the tasks below.

1. **B1, a caught panic must not break the terminal** (P6c-T2, D94). `ratatui::init` installs a panic hook that restores the terminal, and a hook runs before the unwind, on every thread, even when the panic is caught. A panic in the runner on its worker thread (caught and reported as the run's end) left the TUI in a cooked terminal without the alternate screen, with the default report printed over it. `terminal::run` now takes the hook from before `ratatui::init` and replaces ratatui's with its own: on the render thread it restores the terminal and then reports; on any other thread it does nothing, and the thread's own catch brings the message to the app. The decision is a plain function, `terminal::on_panic`, with its test (`only_the_render_thread_s_panic_restores_the_terminal`).
2. **B2, a panicking job reports its failed state** (P6c-T2, and each task that adds a job). The driver runs every job under `catch_unwind`; `Job::on_failure` gives, before the job runs, the result to send if it panics: the failed state of what it works on (a check's `Err`, the update check's `Failed`, the dry run's `Err`, the journal scan's and the preview's `Err`). CHANGES falls back to the planned diff; `[d]`'s result became a `Result`, and its failure shows in the console. The match is exhaustive, so a new job cannot forget its arm. Test: `a_job_that_panics_comes_back_in_its_failed_state` (a panicking update check shows "update check failed"). The worker puts the panic's message into the run's end.
3. **R1, `R` in Plans opens Inspect** (Rens). A run is always preceded by a visible dry run and policy: `R` in Plans opens the plan in Inspect with its dry run starting (the footer says `R run…`), `R` in Inspect starts the run once the dry run is there, and `shift+R` runs from the selected task. `docs/tui-design.md` §6.A says so (P6c-T1). The first version made `R` in Plans start the run at once; that is gone.
4. **M1, the interrupt handling is installed once** (P6c-T2, P6c-T4). `install_interrupt_handler` is called once in `terminal::run`, before `ratatui::init` (the `[o]` shell's `InterruptsIgnored` relies on it), and no more at each run's start on the worker thread: on Windows each call added the console control handler again. Its doc ("once, from the main thread") is now true of every caller, and stays as it is.
5. **Agreed as written:** the stated decisions below, 12 included (`a` retags without running the dry run again, since `dry_run` takes no policy; §6.B now says so, P6c-T1).
6. **Decided, no change to this plan:** UTC for v1 (D94); local time in History goes on the polish list in the handover. The split of inspect.rs, run.rs and dock.rs into state and drawing is the first task of the polish phase, in the handover (After execution, step 4).

## Stated decisions made while building

1. **One plan, nine tasks** (Rens' answer to the handover's question). The decisions come first (P6c-T1), before any code, and the description of the code last (P6c-T9), as in P5 and P6b. Every task between leaves a `planrun tui` that does what it has so far.
2. **The acceptance runs in-process** (D93, Rens' answer). crossterm reads keys from the console, so the binary cannot be driven through a pipe; `driver::Core` runs the same loop over ratatui's `TestBackend` with scripted keys and the real runner on a greeter copy. planrun-cli's `e2e` feature and `PLANRUN_E2E_ABORT` are not used by the TUI; a crash is a panic from a journal observer handed to the next run with `App::observe_next_run`, as planrun-exec's resume tests crash. What only a real terminal shows is the README's manual check list (After execution, step 1).
3. **`docs/plans` is the default plans directory** (D94, Rens' answer), under the repository's root; an argument is relative to the current directory. A directory that does not exist is shown in the Plans view, not refused. Every `*.md` is listed; one no format detects is "not a plan" (this repository's `docs/plans/README.md` is one).
4. **No terminal, no TUI** (D94): stdin and stdout must be terminals, or `planrun tui` exits 2 before raw mode and names `planrun run` as the line version. planrun-cli's `tests/cli.rs` checks it.
5. **Keys on Windows** (D94): only presses count, and a held key's repeats only for movement. One loop polls crossterm's events for 50 ms and drains the app's channel between polls; an input thread would read the keys meant for the `[o]` shell. After the shell returns, keys typed meanwhile are drained. The plan's sessions run with `CREATE_NO_WINDOW` and piped stdio (`shell/kill.rs`), so they never touch the console's mode.
6. **Dependencies.** ratatui 0.30.2, the latest, with `default-features = false` and its `crossterm` feature (no widgets the TUI does not use, no layout cache, no macros). crossterm 0.29.0 comes through `ratatui::crossterm`, so the workspace has no crossterm line of its own and the two can never disagree on a version; D92's crossterm row stands for it. unicode-width 0.2.3 was in `Cargo.lock` already (through annotate-snippets). planrun-tui does not use `similar`: its diffs are the plan's own patch text and `git diff` output, parsed by `diff::parse`. planrun-exec's dependencies and its `unsafe` surface do not change (D92), and the crate is `#![forbid(unsafe_code)]`.
7. **v1 is 24-bit colour, always** (D94): no detection of `COLORTERM` or `WT_SESSION` until the 256-colour fallback exists (§11). The tokens keep their 256 indices (`theme::Token`), pinned by a test.
8. **Times are UTC** (D94): std has no time zone, and a time-zone crate for a label is not worth a D9 line. History's dates say so; the console shows the time since the run started (`mm:ss`, as the 80 × 24 artboard does), which also keeps the tests' snapshots stable.
9. **The glyphs** are §5's, plus the chrome's (`› │ ─ ▎ ▌ ▐ ▾ ▸ ⇣ ← ◂ → … ↑ █ ▪ ▒ ░ ⏎ ▏`), all in one enum with an ASCII fallback each; `·`, whose fallback §5 leaves open, falls back to `.`, so `PLANRUN_ASCII=1` leaves no character outside ASCII that the TUI draws itself. A test pins every glyph and fallback to one cell (`unicode-width`); ⚑ and ⏎ are one cell there.
10. **Snapshots are cell grids** (`grid::grid`): each row's text, then its style runs by token name. They come from the synthetic plans on a greeter copy, with a fixed clock, fixed footer labels and the plan files' times set to the clock's. The resume preview's snapshot replaces the fixture's HEAD (its commit time differs per run) and the plan's fingerprint (a checkout with `core.autocrlf=true` changes the plan's bytes) by placeholders of the same width.
11. **The navigator leaves Notes out**, and a step of Notes only: the artboards show none, and they are prose. The step's own panel lists them.
12. **Inspect's assumed policy changes the tags and `R`, not the dry run.** §6.B says the dry run reruns when `a` changes the policy; the dry run's verdicts do not depend on the policy (`preflight::dry_run` takes none), so the tags are computed again from `Policy::decide` and the dry run is not run again (§6.B says so since P6c-T1). `R` in Plans opens Inspect (review ruling 3); `R` there runs once the dry run is there, and `shift+R` runs from the selected task.
13. **The planned diff comes from the plan alone** (no read of the disk on the render thread): a full-contents write is its lines as added (an overwrite says so above them), a patch its hunks with line numbers, a replacement its before and after without line numbers, a delete a note. In Run, CHANGES shows `git diff HEAD` of the task's paths once a step wrote them and the planned diff before; a committed file shows its planned diff with the COMMITTED chip. The split view (`s`, from 160 columns) is not built: the band says `unified` only.
14. **The dock shows what the run found, then the runner's own words.** Each `QuestionKind` has its layout (the Destructive script's block and its if yes / if skip / if quit, the mismatch's expected lines against the output, the Manual's text quoted, …), and below it `Question.text`, so nothing the runner says is lost. Keys are `Choice::key`, enter for the empty one; an answer the question does not offer does nothing. A typed question takes the whole word and enter; a word it does not accept is shown as refused and nothing is sent.
15. **`q` at a commit's question needs pressing twice** (the P5→P6 handover's item 8, which says the dock must not make it easier to hit): the first `q` says that quitting after the commit was made loses it for resume, and to answer `y`. Every other question quits on the first `q`.
16. **Ctrl-C** opens an END PLANRUN confirm in the dock's place during a run; `y` or a second Ctrl-C restores the terminal and calls `shell::end_now(130)` (D91); `n` or esc keeps running. With no run, Ctrl-C quits. `q` quits only from Plans with no run active (§7).
17. **`[v]` and `[d]`** are answered at once and shown in the upper right panel (the action in full; `git diff HEAD` and the untracked files, read by a job); the dock stays open (D90). **`[o]`** is the driver's: the terminal leaves the alternate screen and raw mode, pwsh (bash on Unix) runs at the root with `InterruptsIgnored` (D73), and the screen is restored and redrawn before the runner hears back.
18. **A run of another planrun is seen through `lock.live`** every two seconds (D90), with the files' stamps. The TUI's own run holds that lock too, so while it runs the header shows the run's own state, not "running in pid N". History shows a run without `run-end` as running while a run is live and as killed otherwise.
19. **Resume from the preview** uses the journaled policy and range, as `planrun resume` without flags does; Runner::resume takes the lock and checks everything again (D77, D85).
20. **app.rs and plans.rs pass 400 lines**, so their tests move to `app_tests.rs` and `plans_tests.rs` (CLAUDE.md), in P6c-T8, the task that adds the app's test. inspect.rs (about 1000 lines), run.rs (about 1250) and dock.rs (about 700) are large, with no tests of their own inside; their split into state and drawing is the polish phase's first task (review ruling 6).
21. **Panics stay on their thread** (review rulings 1 and 2, D94): the render thread's restores the terminal and reports; the runner's and a job's are caught by their thread and come back as that work's failure, with the panic's message (`jobs::panic_message`).

## Tasks

### P6c-T1: the TUI's test and start-up decisions

**Goal:** The decisions agreed in writing before any code: D93 (the TUI is tested in-process, amending D82) and D94 (how `planrun tui` starts and reads keys, installs interrupts once and handles a panic, amending D89) at the end of docs/decisions.md, their amended-by lines in D82 and D89, both in `docs/tui-design.md` §1 and its §11's acceptance, §6.A's `R` (it opens Inspect) and §6.B's `a` (it retags; the dry run is not run again), and the roadmap's P6c with this plan.

**Files**

- Modify `docs/decisions.md`
- Modify `docs/roadmap.md`
- Modify `docs/tui-design.md`

Over 300 lines, so they come as exact diffs: `docs/decisions.md`, `docs/tui-design.md`.

**Step 1 — write the files**

--- a/docs/decisions.md
+++ b/docs/decisions.md
@@ -1202,6 +1202,8 @@ k-th write), `commit-start` (after a Commit's `action-start`). It ends with `exi
 for the test machine to answer. A kill of `git commit` itself (HEAD moved, `action-end` missing)
 is tested in planrun-exec, where the observer can make the commit before it crashes.
 
+Amended by D93 (the TUI is tested in-process).
+
 ## D83 Releases, install scripts and `planrun update`
 
 planrun is installed and kept current from GitHub releases; `Renseck/planrun` is public, so no
@@ -1337,6 +1339,8 @@ Amends D15.
   Terminal with Cascadia Mono; each has an ASCII fallback, and `PLANRUN_ASCII=1` switches to them

+Amended by D94 (v1 renders 24-bit colour without detecting it).
+
 ## D90 The runner stays synchronous, on a worker thread
 
 Amends D15, D76 and D77.
@@ -1384,3 +1388,52 @@ Amends D9. `ratatui`, `crossterm` and `unicode-width`, in planrun-tui only. cros
 brings `windows-sys`, `mio` and `signal-hook`, which D64 and D73 keep out of planrun-exec; they
 stay out of it: planrun-exec's dependencies and its `unsafe` surface (`shell/kill.rs`) do not
 change.
+
+## D93 The TUI is tested in-process
+
+Amends D82 for the TUI.
+
+- The TUI cannot be driven through pipes: crossterm reads keys from the console (on Windows
+  `ReadConsoleInput`), so driving the binary would need a pseudo-console, which means a new
+  dependency or `unsafe` code outside `shell/kill.rs`. Instead, planrun-tui's loop is one core
+  (`driver::Core`) over any ratatui backend, fed keys, ticks and the messages of its threads.
+  The binary gives it crossterm's terminal and events; the tests a `TestBackend` and scripted
+  keys.
+- **Its acceptance** runs the real runner on a copy of the greeter fixture
+  (`tests/support/fixture.rs`) through that core: the synthetic assay plan is inspected, run,
+  answered in the dock and committed, and the journal and the tree are checked as P5's binary
+  test checks them. A crash is a panic from a journal observer, as in planrun-exec's resume
+  tests, and the resume preview and resume are driven from the journal it leaves.
+- **Snapshots are cell grids.** `grid::grid` prints each row's characters and then its style runs
+  by token name (`act/bg2 b`), so a snapshot reads against the design's artboards cell for
+  cell. Times come from a fixed clock, and the footer's target and plans labels are fixed.
+- What only a real terminal shows (the glyphs in Cascadia Mono, the keys, the `[o]` shell, Ctrl-C,
+  a resize, the terminal restored after a panic) is a manual check list in the crate's README.
+
+## D94 How `planrun tui` starts and reads keys
+
+Amends D89.
+
+- **A terminal on stdin and stdout**, or it stops before raw mode with exit code 2 and says
+  that `planrun run` is the line version.
+- **The repository is the current directory's** (D88). The plans directory is the argument,
+  relative to the current directory, or `docs/plans` under the repository's root; a missing
+  directory is shown in the Plans view, not refused. Its `*.md` files are listed, not
+  recursively; a file no format detects is listed as "not a plan".
+- **Keys:** only presses count (Windows reports releases too), and a held key's repeats only for
+  movement. One loop polls crossterm's events and drains the app's channel between polls: no
+  input thread, which would read the keys meant for the `[o]` shell. Ctrl-C is a key in raw mode
+  (D91). VS Code's terminal keeps `ctrl+k` and `ctrl+p` for itself unless
+  `terminal.integrated.sendKeybindingsToShell` is set; Windows Terminal passes them.
+- **Colour:** v1 renders 24-bit colour without looking at `COLORTERM` or `WT_SESSION`; the
+  detection comes with the 256-colour fallback.
+- **Times are UTC**, marked so where a date is shown: std has no time zone, and a time-zone crate
+  for one label is not worth a D9 line. The console shows the time since the run started.
+- **The plan files are checked in the background** on start, every two seconds when a file's
+  length or modification time changed, and on `c`.
+- **Interrupts** (D73) are installed once, before the terminal is set up, not at each run: the
+  `[o]` shell's `InterruptsIgnored` relies on them.
+- **A panic** on the render thread restores the terminal, then reports as Rust does. On any
+  other thread (the runner's, a background job's) it leaves the terminal alone and prints
+  nothing: the thread catches it, and its message comes back to the app as that work's failure
+  (a run that ended with an error, a job in its failed state).
```

git apply --whitespace=nowarn "$env:TEMP\p6c-t1-docs-decisions-md.patch"
Remove-Item "$env:TEMP\p6c-t1-docs-decisions-md.patch"
```

File `docs/roadmap.md` (full contents):

`````markdown
# Roadmap

Each phase gets its own plan in `docs/plans/` (conventions: [plans/README.md](plans/README.md)),
reviewed by Rens before it is executed. A phase is done when its acceptance list holds and
`cargo fmt --check`, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` and `cargo test` are green.

The order puts the riskiest assumption first: that real plans can be parsed strictly, with full
accounting of every block.

## P0 — Starter package (done)

Workspace of four crates; the IR in `planrun-model` with its tests; the parse and exec extension
points as traits; the CLI argument surface; `formats/assay.toml`; the synthetic and invalid
corpus; the greeter fixture; these docs.

## P1 — Parse the assay format strictly (done)

The deliverable: `planrun check` and `planrun show` on assay plans, with precise diagnostics.

Suggested tasks (the plan may split or merge them):

1. **Source and document.** `source::load` (UTF-8, ≤ 64 MiB, BOM, CRLF/CR → LF, SHA-256 of the
   original bytes; adds `sha2`, D9). The document builder over pulldown-cmark's offset iterator:
   every `Block` variant, spans, list depth, `TextRef::Owned` for fences inside list items, and
   the `unclosed-fence` error. Unit tests on small strings, including a ```` ```` ```` fence that
   contains ```` ``` ```` fences, a fence inside a list item, CRLF input, and an empty fence body.
2. **Specs, registry, format selection, outline.** `Registry::add_spec` (deserialize, compile
   every regex, build recognizers through factories, reject unknown kinds and duplicate ids),
   `with_builtins` embedding `formats/assay.toml`, the generic `SpecFormat` (`detect`, `outline`:
   tasks after `tasks_after`, task ids and ordinals, phase, intro step, manifest, step ranges,
   `tasks_end_level`), selection with threshold and ties (`no-format`, `ambiguous-format`).
3. **Recognition.** The cursor loop with `No`/`Yes`/`Amend`/`Malformed` and the fallback; the
   kinds the assay spec uses: `file-block`, `ceremony`, `delete`, `generated`, `script`,
   `expected-block`, `expected-prose`. Patch parsing for `ApplyPatch` (target path from
   `diff --git`/`---`/`+++` agrees with the lead: `patch-path`; the `index` line → pre/post).
4. **Checks.** The rules `task-order` (with `duplicate-task-id`), `step-order`, `manifest`
   (`manifest-undeclared`, `manifest-untouched`, `manifest-write-mode`), `patch-shape`,
   `blob-chain`, `commit` (`commit-twice`, `commit-not-last`); the coverage report. The
   invalid-corpus test (`tests/corpus/invalid/README.md`), which also pins the codes of the
   outline and the recognizers (`bad-path`, `unknown-file-op`, `missing-block`, `patch-path`, …).
5. **CLI.** `planrun check` (diagnostics via `annotate-snippets`, coverage table, exit 0 / 1 on
   errors / 2 on usage) and `planrun show` (plan, task, task/step). An `insta` snapshot of the IR
   of `assay-style.md` and of `planrun check` output. The `#[ignore]`d local-corpus test.

Acceptance:

- `planrun check tests/corpus/synthetic/assay-style.md` exits 0; every block of every task is an
  action except the `**Goal:**` lines and prose (Notes); the absorbed ceremonies are listed as
  absorbed.
- Every `tests/corpus/invalid/*.md` yields exactly its pinned error code.
- The two local assay plans (`2026-11-05-s5-1-p1-routes-seam.md`,
  `2026-11-11-s5-1-p3c1-builder-core.md`) parse with zero errors. Expect to widen the `generated`
  pattern for "`Cargo.lock` changes: it is regenerated by cargo in the next step", and to see
  the P3c-1 plan's prose-heavy preamble as Notes.
- Every diagnostic shows file:line:col and the source line.

## P2 — The other three formats (done)

The decisions are D26–D40. Split in two plans on one schema (D40):

- **P2a (done).** The P1 outline fixes and `task-after-end` (D37); the spec schema (`required`,
  a marker that captures `change`, `siblings`, `ChangeKind::Generated`, `[checks]`,
  `[[step_marks]]`, `script` `leads`/`first_block`/intent `lead`); the kinds `run-line`,
  `before-after`, `manual-prose`; path resolution from the manifest (D32);
  `formats/superpowers.toml` and `before-after.toml` with IR snapshots of their synthetic plans.
  The superpowers and before-after local plans parse with zero errors. Plan:
  `docs/plans/2026-11-24-p2a-hand-written-formats.md`.
- **P2b (done).** First, pattern manifest entries: a range or glob item is one entry whose
  target is a `PathPattern` (D41, D42). Then the schema for hand-written DevReady plans: inline
  Files clauses (D43), a change per path (D44), Bare file names (D45), kept step labels (D46),
  `Expected:` with a list (D47), comment expectations that a sentence extends (D35, D48),
  numbered edit series (D49). Then `formats/devready.toml` with the `**Commit:**` Manual (D33),
  detection that outscores superpowers on both devready plans, the IR snapshot of its synthetic
  plan, and the Apply invariant in plan-grammar §5. All six local plans parse with zero errors.
  Plan: `docs/plans/2026-11-25-p2b-devready-format.md`.

Each plan lists the imperative Notes that remain in the real plans (D36). `expected-failure-step`
became step marks (D30); `preview-apply` became script intents by lead (D29).

## P3 — Workspace, git and dry-run (done)

`RepoPath` resolution with the symlink check; the per-task file cache; step transactions (D23);
line-ending policy (D10); pre-images in the git object database; the `git` wrapper (`apply
--check`/`apply` on stdin, `hash-object --path`, `status --porcelain`, `cat-file`); blob-id checks;
task preflight; `planrun run --dry-run` against a virtual tree. Integration tests on a temporary
copy of `tests/fixtures/greeter`: the dry run of `assay-style.md` passes; a fixture with
`src/lib.rs` edited fails preflight with the blob mismatch; a Create onto an existing file fails
before anything is written.

The decisions are D50–D59. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-11-25-p3-workspace-dry-run.md`. First the P2b
review items (series stems D59, `run-from`, negated failures, `write-mode-unknown` D58) and the
`RepoPath` hardening (D51); then the git wrapper, the workspace and its one writer, line endings
(D54), the virtual tree (D52), step transactions as prepare and commit (D53), preflight with
scope (D50) and the dry run (D55). The per-task file cache is the virtual tree: a task's paths are
read once, on first use.

## P4 — Shells, expectations, policy (done)

Persistent pwsh and bash sessions (D24) with the sentinel protocol: variables persist across
blocks, a `throw` ends the block and not the session, output streams. The expectation matcher
(summary-line subsequence with `…`, exit expectations, prose → confirmation, D11). The command
classifier (D13). Expected failures skipped by default (D12). Tests for each, with pwsh and bash
when present (skipped with a message when not).

The decisions are D60–D70. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-11-25-p4-shells-expectations-policy.md`. First
the decisions and the `unsafe` lints (D64); then the sessions (the protocol D60, how a block
ends D61, the environment D62, which shells D63, the process tree D64); the matcher (D66); the
classifier (D68) and the policy decisions (D67, D69); the classes in the dry run. The candidate
(a script working directory from a run lead) is deferred (D70). The P4 review items P5 must fix
first are in `docs/handovers/2026-11-25-p4-to-p5.md`.

## P5 — Runner, journal, resume, interactive CLI (done)

The runner state machine; the commit gate (D22); the JSONL journal (D18); resume with `DoneState`
(D14); `run`, `resume`, `status`; the line-prompt UI (D15) with view/diff/shell/skip/quit. End to
end: `planrun run tests/corpus/synthetic/assay-style.md --auto read,build,modify` on a fixture copy
produces the same two commits (same trees) as the fixture's history; killing the process mid-task
and resuming completes it without repeating a write.

The decisions are D71–D82. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-11-25-p5-runner-journal-resume.md`. First the
P4 review items for sessions (D71) and sed (D72); then the transaction hardening and the step's
state on disk (D78), the journal and the lock (D76, D77), the runner with the commit gate and the
checks around a commit (D73–D75, D79–D81), resume (D77), and the CLI with line prompts (D74).
The fixture has no history of its own: the acceptance test builds the two expected trees, and
drives the binary through the test-only `e2e` feature (D82), uninterrupted, killed at a
question, and ended at three seams. The synthetic plan writes `docs/modules.txt` with LF only,
and the greeter ignores `Cargo.lock`.

## P6 — Dogfood and polish

planrun runs its own later phase plans. Candidates, each its own decision: a ratatui UI, a
sidecar IR from planbuild.py, `.planrun/formats/` discovery polish, a non-interactive batch mode
for CI.

- **P6a — release and update (done).** The CI gate on Windows and Linux, the release workflow on
  a `v*.*.*` tag, `scripts/install.ps1` and `install.sh` (a release, or `--from-source`), and
  `planrun update [--check]` (D83). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-11-26-p6a-release-update.md`; the
  first plan planrun executes on itself. Open items: `docs/handovers/2026-11-26-p5-to-p6.md`.
- **P6b — the TUI's prerequisites.** The TUI's design (`docs/tui-design.md`) and its decisions
  (D84–D92), then what it needs from planrun-exec (its §9): a journal with any number of
  observers, `shell::end_now`, typed effects in the dry-run report, the matcher's alignment,
  `QuestionKind`, and the read-only resume preview. The CLI's behaviour and the journal format do
  not change. Plan: `docs/plans/2026-11-26-p6b-tui-prerequisites.md`.
- **P6c — the TUI.** The `planrun-tui` crate and `planrun tui`: theme and glyphs, the shell, the
  background work, Plans, the plan view (Inspect, then Run through its `Ui` and a journal
  observer), the dock with every `QuestionKind`, History, the resume preview and the command
  palette (`docs/tui-design.md` §11). Its acceptance drives the TUI against the greeter fixture
  in-process, with the real runner (D93). Plan: `docs/plans/2026-11-27-p6c-tui.md`.
`````

**Step 2 — verify**

Targeted: D93 and D94 are there once each, and so are their amended-by lines:

```powershell
(Select-String -Path docs/decisions.md -Pattern '^## D9[34] ').Count
(Select-String -Path docs/decisions.md -Pattern 'Amended by D9[34]').Count
```

Expected (exit code 0; summary lines):

```text
2
2
```

Gate:

```powershell
$env:PLANRUN_REQUIRE_SHELLS = '1'; cargo fmt --check; if ($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0) { cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings }; if ($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0) { cargo clippy -p planrun-cli --all-targets --features e2e -- -D warnings }; if ($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0) { cargo test }; if ($LASTEXITCODE -eq 0) { cargo test -p planrun-cli --features e2e --test run }; $gate = $LASTEXITCODE; "gate exit code: $gate"
```

Expected (exit code 0; summary lines):

```text
gate exit code: 0
```

**Step 3 — commit**

Only the task's paths are staged, only when the gate's exit code above was 0, and only when every staged file is the verified one:

```powershell
if ($gate -eq 0) {
  git add -- docs/decisions.md docs/roadmap.md docs/tui-design.md
  $verified = [ordered]@{
      'docs/decisions.md' = '7d433e79a6612c5ec95c7dd60ff4428d898a0c73'
      'docs/roadmap.md' = '2a10821fa22fe1bfebb98f2d8d8280cb287c8222'
      'docs/tui-design.md' = '881dd738507d2f70f0b5d8ac383a30f6fe050a9f'
  }
  $differ = @($verified.Keys | Where-Object { (git rev-parse ":$_") -ne $verified[$_] })
  if ($differ.Count -eq 0) { git commit -m "P6c-T1: the TUI's test and start-up decisions" }
  else { "staged files differ from the verified tree: $($differ -join ', ')" }
} else { "the gate failed (exit code $gate): fix the task before committing" }
git status --porcelain
```

Expected: the commit, then no output from `git status --porcelain`. A line naming files instead of the commit means those files differ from the verified tree.

### P6c-T2: the crate, the shell and the Plans view

**Goal:** `planrun tui` shows the Plans view: the crate with its colour tokens, glyphs and the cell-grid snapshot format; the app, the driver (one loop over any backend) and the crossterm terminal; background jobs (the plans directory's scan, each plan's check and journal state, `lock.live`, the update check), each run under `catch_unwind` so that a panic comes back as its failed state; the panic hook, which restores the terminal only for the render thread's panic, and the interrupt handling, installed once; the header, the footer and the size gate; the Plans table, its filter and the selected plan's detail (outline with coverage strips, CHECK, STATUS, PREFLIGHT). planrun-cli gains `tui [<plans-dir>]` and `update::state` for the indicator.

```powershell
git apply --whitespace=nowarn "$env:TEMP\p6c-t2-cargo-lock.patch"
Remove-Item "$env:TEMP\p6c-t2-cargo-lock.patch"
```

File `Cargo.toml` (full contents):

```toml
[workspace]
resolver = "3"
members = ["crates/*"]
# The fixture repos are inputs of the tests, not workspace members.
exclude = ["tests/fixtures"]

[workspace.package]
version = "0.1.0"
edition = "2024"
license = "MIT"
publish = false

[workspace.dependencies]
planrun-model = { path = "crates/planrun-model" }
planrun-parse = { path = "crates/planrun-parse" }
planrun-exec = { path = "crates/planrun-exec" }
planrun-tui = { path = "crates/planrun-tui" }

# Each dependency is justified in docs/decisions.md (D9). Add none without a line there.
annotate-snippets = "0.12"
clap = { version = "4.6", features = ["derive"] }
insta = { version = "1.49", features = ["yaml"] }
pulldown-cmark = { version = "0.13", default-features = false }
# The TUI's terminal comes through ratatui's re-export (`ratatui::crossterm`, crossterm 0.29),
# so the two never disagree on a version (D92).
ratatui = { version = "0.30.2", default-features = false, features = ["crossterm"] }
regex = "1.13"
serde = { version = "1.0", features = ["derive"] }
serde_json = "1.0"
sha1_smol = "1.0"
sha2 = "0.11"
similar = "3.2"
thiserror = "2.0"
toml = "1.2"
unicode-width = "0.2.3"

[workspace.lints.rust]
unsafe_code = "deny"
missing_docs = "warn"

[workspace.lints.clippy]
all = { level = "warn", priority = -1 }
unwrap_used = "warn"
expect_used = "warn"
undocumented_unsafe_blocks = "deny"
```

File `crates/planrun-cli/Cargo.toml` (full contents):

```toml
[package]
name = "planrun-cli"
description = "The planrun binary: check, show, run, resume, status"
version.workspace = true
edition.workspace = true
license.workspace = true
publish.workspace = true

[[bin]]
name = "planrun"
path = "src/main.rs"

[dependencies]
annotate-snippets.workspace = true
clap.workspace = true
planrun-exec.workspace = true
planrun-model.workspace = true
planrun-parse.workspace = true

+/// `planrun tui`: exit 0 when it quits, 2 when it cannot start (no terminal, no repository).
+fn tui(plans_dir: Option<PathBuf>, out: &mut String) -> u8 {
+    let dir = match std::env::current_dir() {
+        Ok(dir) => dir,
+        Err(e) => {
+            out.push_str(&format!("planrun: the current directory: {e}\n"));
+            return USAGE;
+        }
+    };
+    let host = planrun_tui::Host {
+        dir,
+        plans: plans_dir,
+        version: env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION").to_owned(),
+        update: Some(Box::new(update::state)),
+    };
+    match planrun_tui::run(host) {
+        Ok(()) => OK,
+        Err(e) => {
+            out.push_str(&format!("planrun: {e}\n"));
+            USAGE
+        }
+    }
+}

+/// The update check for the TUI's indicator (D83): what `planrun update --check` would say.
+pub fn state() -> planrun_tui::jobs::UpdateState {
+    use planrun_tui::jobs::UpdateState;
+    let Some(installed) = Version::parse(env!("CARGO_PKG_VERSION")) else {
+        return UpdateState::Failed;
+    };
+    let latest = Tools::find()
+        .and_then(|tools| tools.fetch(LATEST))
+        .and_then(|json| Release::from_json(&json));
+    match latest {
+        Ok(release) if release.version > installed => {
+            UpdateState::Available(release.version.to_string())
+        }
+        Ok(_) => UpdateState::Current,
+        Err(_) => UpdateState::Failed,
+    }

```rust
//! The `planrun` binary: exit codes, `check` output, the rendered message of every invalid
//! plan, and `show` of one step. Runs from the workspace root with relative paths, so the output
//! is the same on every machine.

use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
use std::process::Command;

fn root() -> PathBuf {
    Path::new(env!("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")).join("../..")
}

/// Runs `planrun args…`; returns the exit code and stdout + stderr.
fn planrun(args: &[&str]) -> (i32, String) {
    let output = Command::new(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_planrun"))
        .args(args)
        .current_dir(root())
        .output()
        .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{e}"));
    let mut text = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).into_owned();
    text.push_str(&String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr));
    (output.status.code().unwrap_or(-1), text)
}

const SYNTHETIC: &str = "tests/corpus/synthetic/assay-style.md";

#[test]
fn check_passes_the_synthetic_plan() {
    let (code, out) = planrun(&["check", SYNTHETIC]);
    assert_eq!(code, 0, "{out}");
    insta::assert_snapshot!("check_assay_style", out);
}

#[test]
fn check_renders_every_invalid_plan() {
    let dir = root().join("tests/corpus/invalid");
    let mut names: Vec<String> = std::fs::read_dir(&dir)
        .unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{e}"))
        .map(|e| {
            e.unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("{e}"))
                .file_name()
                .to_string_lossy()
                .into_owned()
        })
        .filter(|n| n.ends_with(".md") && n != "README.md")
        .collect();
    names.sort();
    let mut all = String::new();
    for name in &names {
        let path = format!("tests/corpus/invalid/{name}");
        let (code, out) = planrun(&["check", &path]);
        assert_eq!(code, 1, "{path}: {out}");
        all.push_str(&format!("==== {name}\n{out}\n"));
    }
    insta::assert_snapshot!("check_invalid_corpus", all);
}

#[test]
fn show_prints_a_step_in_full() {
    let (code, out) = planrun(&["show", SYNTHETIC, "P1-T2/2"]);
    assert_eq!(code, 0, "{out}");
    insta::assert_snapshot!("show_p1_t2_step_2", out);
}

#[test]
fn usage_problems_exit_with_2() {
    assert_eq!(planrun(&["check"]).0, 2);
    assert_eq!(planrun(&["check", "tests/corpus/none.md"]).0, 2);
    assert_eq!(planrun(&["show", SYNTHETIC, "P9-T9"]).0, 2);
    assert_eq!(planrun(&["show", SYNTHETIC, "P1-T1/7"]).0, 2);
    assert_eq!(planrun(&["check", SYNTHETIC, "--format", "nope"]).0, 1);
}

#[test]
fn tui_refuses_a_stdout_that_is_not_a_terminal() {
    let (code, out) = planrun(&["tui"]);
    assert_eq!(code, 2, "{out}");
    assert!(out.contains("planrun tui needs a terminal"), "{out}");
}

File `crates/planrun-tui/src/app.rs` (full contents):

```rust
//! The app: all of the TUI's state, the keys and messages that change it, and the effects it
//! asks the driver for. It never blocks: git, shells and the network are background jobs or the
//! runner's worker thread (D90). Drawing reads the state and changes nothing.

use std::sync::Arc;

use ratatui::buffer::Buffer;
use ratatui::layout::Rect;
use ratatui::style::Style;

use crate::chrome::{self, MIN, Nav};
use crate::draw::fill;
use crate::glyph::{Glyph, Glyphs};
use crate::jobs::{Context, Done, Job, UpdateCheck, UpdateState};
use crate::keys::Key;
use crate::plans::{self, Labels, Plans};
use crate::theme::{Token, base, on};

/// Milliseconds since the Unix epoch; the tests fix it.
pub type Clock = Arc<dyn Fn() -> u64 + Send + Sync>;

/// The system clock.
pub fn system_clock() -> Clock {
    Arc::new(|| {
        std::time::SystemTime::now()
            .duration_since(std::time::UNIX_EPOCH)
            .map_or(0, |d| u64::try_from(d.as_millis()).unwrap_or(u64::MAX))
    })
}

/// How often the plans directory is scanned and `lock.live` probed.
const POLL_MS: u64 = 2000;

/// Which view the body shows.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum View {
    /// F2.
    Plans,
}

/// What the app asks the driver to do.
pub enum Effect {
    /// Run a job on a thread of its own.
    Job(Job),
    /// Stop.
    Quit,
}

/// What reaches the app from its threads.
pub enum Msg {
    /// A background job's result.
    Done(Done),
}

/// The TUI's state.
pub struct App {
    pub(crate) ctx: Arc<Context>,
    pub(crate) g: Glyphs,
    pub(crate) clock: Clock,
    pub(crate) view: View,
    pub(crate) plans: Plans,
    pub(crate) update: UpdateState,
    pub(crate) update_check: Option<UpdateCheck>,
    /// A run of another planrun holds the lock, with `lock.pid`'s pid (D90).
    pub(crate) live: Option<Option<u32>>,
    pub(crate) next_poll: u64,
    pub(crate) frame: u8,
    pub(crate) quit: bool,
}

impl App {
    /// The app for the repository `ctx` describes; `update` is the update check, if any.
    pub fn new(ctx: Context, g: Glyphs, clock: Clock, update: Option<UpdateCheck>) -> App {
        App {
            ctx: Arc::new(ctx),
            g,
            clock,
            view: View::Plans,
            plans: Plans::default(),
            update: UpdateState::Unchecked,
            update_check: update,
            live: None,
            next_poll: 0,
            frame: 0,
            quit: false,
        }
    }

    /// The context jobs run in.
    pub fn context(&self) -> Arc<Context> {
        self.ctx.clone()
    }

    /// Whether the app is done.
    pub fn done(&self) -> bool {
        self.quit
    }

    /// The Plans view's state.
    pub fn plans(&self) -> &Plans {
        &self.plans
    }

    /// What the update check found.
    pub fn update(&self) -> &UpdateState {
        &self.update
    }

    /// The jobs to start with.
    pub fn start(&mut self) -> Vec<Effect> {
        self.next_poll = (self.clock)() + POLL_MS;
        let mut effects = vec![Effect::Job(Job::Scan), Effect::Job(Job::Live)];
        if let Some(check) = self.update_check.take() {
            effects.push(Effect::Job(Job::Update(check)));
        }
        effects
    }

    /// Time passes: the spinner turns, and every two seconds the plans directory is scanned and
    /// `lock.live` probed.
    pub fn tick(&mut self) -> Vec<Effect> {
        let now = (self.clock)();
        self.frame = u8::try_from((now / 250) % 4).unwrap_or(0);
        if now < self.next_poll {
            return Vec::new();
        }
        self.next_poll = now + POLL_MS;
        vec![Effect::Job(Job::Scan), Effect::Job(Job::Live)]
    }

    /// A message from a thread.
    pub fn msg(&mut self, msg: Msg) -> Vec<Effect> {
        match msg {
            Msg::Done(done) => self.done_job(done),
        }
    }

    fn done_job(&mut self, done: Done) -> Vec<Effect> {
        match done {
            Done::Scanned(files) => self
                .plans
                .scanned(files)
                .into_iter()
                .map(Effect::Job)
                .collect(),
            Done::Checked { path, stamp, file } => {
                self.plans.checked(&path, stamp, file);
                Vec::new()
            }
            Done::Live(live) => {
                self.live = live.unwrap_or(None);
                Vec::new()
            }
            Done::Update(state) => {
                self.update = state;
                Vec::new()
            }
        }
    }

    /// A key.
    pub fn key(&mut self, key: Key) -> Vec<Effect> {
        match key {
            Key::F(2) => {
                self.view = View::Plans;
                return Vec::new();
            }
            Key::Ctrl('c') if self.view == View::Plans => return vec![Effect::Quit],
            _ => {}
        }
        match self.view {
            View::Plans => self.plans_key(key),
        }
    }

    fn plans_key(&mut self, key: Key) -> Vec<Effect> {
        match self.plans.key(key) {
            plans::Outcome::Check(jobs) => jobs.into_iter().map(Effect::Job).collect(),
            plans::Outcome::Unused if key == Key::Char('q') => vec![Effect::Quit],
            _ => Vec::new(),
        }
    }

    fn labels(&self) -> Labels {
        Labels {
            plans: self.ctx.plans_label.clone(),
            root: self.ctx.root.clone(),
        }
    }

    /// Draws the whole screen.
    pub fn draw(&self, buf: &mut Buffer) {
        let area = buf.area;
        fill(buf, area, base());
        if area.width < MIN.0 || area.height < MIN.1 {
            chrome::too_small(buf, area, self.g);
            return;
        }
        let footer_rows = if area.height >= 30 { 2 } else { 1 };
        let body = Rect::new(
            area.x,
            area.y + 1,
            area.width,
            area.height - 1 - footer_rows,
        );
        let (crumbs, keys) = match self.view {
            View::Plans => (
                vec![self.ctx.repo.clone(), "plans".to_owned()],
                self.plans.footer(),
            ),
        };
        chrome::header(buf, area, &crumbs, &self.state(), self.g);
        match self.view {
            View::Plans => self.plans.draw(buf, body, &self.labels(), self.g),
        }
        if footer_rows == 2 {
            chrome::keys(buf, area, area.bottom() - 2, &keys, self.g);
        }
        chrome::nav(
            buf,
            area,
            area.bottom() - 1,
            self.nav(),
            &self.ctx,
            &self.update,
            self.g,
        );
    }

    fn nav(&self) -> Nav {
        match self.view {
            View::Plans => Nav::Plans,
        }
    }

    /// The header's right side: what planrun is doing, and other runs.
    fn state(&self) -> Vec<(String, Style)> {
        let bar = |t: Token| on(t, Token::Bg1);
        let mut out = vec![
            (format!("{} ", self.g.get(Glyph::Pending)), bar(Token::Fg3)),
            ("idle".to_owned(), bar(Token::Fg2)),
        ];
        let dot = format!("  {}  ", self.g.get(Glyph::Dot));
        match self.live {
            Some(pid) => {
                let pid =
                    pid.map_or_else(|| "running".to_owned(), |p| format!("running in pid {p}"));
                out.push((dot, bar(Token::Fg3)));
                out.push((pid, bar(Token::Fg2)));
            }
            None => {
                let n = self.plans.interrupted();
                if n > 0 {
                    let s = if n == 1 { "" } else { "s" };
                    out.push((dot, bar(Token::Fg3)));
                    out.push((format!("{n} interrupted run{s}"), bar(Token::Warn)));
                }
            }
        }
        out
    }
}
```

File `crates/planrun-tui/src/chrome.rs` (full contents):

```rust
//! The shell every view shares (docs/tui-design.md §3): the header band with the crumbs and the
//! state on the right, the footer's contextual keys (from 30 rows up) and its navigation line
//! with the target, the plans directory and the update indicator pinned bottom-right.

use ratatui::buffer::Buffer;
use ratatui::layout::Rect;
use ratatui::style::Style;

use crate::draw::{Pen, fill};
use crate::glyph::{Glyph, Glyphs};
use crate::jobs::{Context, UpdateState};
use crate::text;
use crate::theme::{Token, bold, on};

/// The smallest terminal v1 lays out (§11): below it the TUI says so.
pub const MIN: (u16, u16) = (100, 30);

/// The views F2–F4 switch between, and F1.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Nav {
    /// F1.
    Help,
    /// F2.
    Plans,
    /// F3.
    Plan,
    /// F4.
    History,
}

/// The header: ` PLANRUN  crumb › crumb › current`, and `right` ending one cell before the edge.
pub fn header(
    buf: &mut Buffer,
    area: Rect,
    crumbs: &[String],
    right: &[(String, Style)],
    g: Glyphs,
) {
    let row = Rect::new(area.x, area.y, area.width, 1);
    fill(buf, row, on(Token::Fg, Token::Bg1));
    let right_w: usize = right.iter().map(|(s, _)| text::width(s)).sum();
    let crumbs_end = area
        .right()
        .saturating_sub(u16::try_from(right_w).unwrap_or(u16::MAX) + 2);
    let mut pen = Pen::new(buf, area.x + 1, area.y, crumbs_end, g);
    pen.text("PLANRUN", bold(Token::Fg).bg(Token::Bg1.color()))
        .gap(2);
    for (i, crumb) in crumbs.iter().enumerate() {
        if i > 0 {
            pen.text(
                &format!(" {} ", g.get(Glyph::Crumb)),
                on(Token::Fg3, Token::Bg1),
            );
        }
        let style = if i + 1 == crumbs.len() {
            bold(Token::Fg).bg(Token::Bg1.color())
        } else {
            on(Token::Fg2, Token::Bg1)
        };
        pen.text(crumb, style);
    }
    let start = area
        .right()
        .saturating_sub(u16::try_from(right_w).unwrap_or(0) + 1);
    let mut pen = Pen::new(buf, start, area.y, area.right().saturating_sub(1), g);
    for (s, style) in right {
        pen.text(s, *style);
    }
}

/// The contextual keys: chips on bg1, three cells apart.
pub fn keys(buf: &mut Buffer, area: Rect, y: u16, keys: &[(String, String)], g: Glyphs) {
    let row = Rect::new(area.x, y, area.width, 1);
    fill(buf, row, on(Token::Fg, Token::Bg1));
    let mut pen = Pen::new(buf, area.x + 1, y, area.right(), g);
    for (i, (key, label)) in keys.iter().enumerate() {
        if i > 0 {
            pen.gap(3);
        }
        pen.chip(key, label, Token::Bg1);
    }
}

/// The navigation line: F1–F4 (the active one in act on bg2), the target and the plans
/// directory, and the update indicator, never green or red.
pub fn nav(
    buf: &mut Buffer,
    area: Rect,
    y: u16,
    active: Nav,
    ctx: &Context,
    update: &UpdateState,
    g: Glyphs,
) {
    let row = Rect::new(area.x, y, area.width, 1);
    fill(buf, row, on(Token::Fg, Token::Bg1));
    let (indicator, style) = indicator(update, &ctx.version, g);
    let end = area
        .right()
        .saturating_sub(u16::try_from(text::width(&indicator)).unwrap_or(0) + 2);
    let mut pen = Pen::new(buf, area.x + 1, y, end, g);
    let tabs = [
        (Nav::Help, "F1 Help"),
        (Nav::Plans, "F2 Plans"),
        (Nav::Plan, "F3 Plan"),
        (Nav::History, "F4 History"),
    ];
    for (i, (nav, label)) in tabs.iter().enumerate() {
        if i > 0 {
            pen.gap(1);
        }
        let style = if *nav == active {
            bold(Token::Act).bg(Token::Bg2.color())
        } else {
            on(Token::Fg3, Token::Bg1)
        };
        pen.text(&format!(" {label} "), style);
    }
    pen.gap(4);
    pen.text("target ", on(Token::Fg3, Token::Bg1))
        .text(&ctx.target, on(Token::Fg2, Token::Bg1))
        .text(
            &format!("  {}  plans ", g.get(Glyph::Dot)),
            on(Token::Fg3, Token::Bg1),
        )
        .text(&ctx.plans_label, on(Token::Fg2, Token::Bg1));
    let mut pen = Pen::new(buf, end, y, area.right().saturating_sub(1), g);
    pen.right(&indicator, style);
}

fn indicator(update: &UpdateState, version: &str, g: Glyphs) -> (String, Style) {
    match update {
        UpdateState::Unchecked | UpdateState::Current => {
            (format!("planrun {version}"), on(Token::Fg3, Token::Bg1))
        }
        UpdateState::Available(v) => (
            format!("{} {v} available", g.get(Glyph::Up)),
            on(Token::Meta, Token::Bg1),
        ),
        UpdateState::Failed => ("update check failed".to_owned(), on(Token::Fg3, Token::Bg1)),
    }
}

/// The notice below 100 × 30 (§11): the run, if any, goes on underneath.
pub fn too_small(buf: &mut Buffer, area: Rect, g: Glyphs) {
    let lines = [
        "terminal too small".to_owned(),
        format!("needs {} {} {}", MIN.0, "×", MIN.1),
        format!("now {} {} {}", area.width, "×", area.height),
    ];
    let top = area.y + area.height / 2 - 1;
    for (i, line) in lines.iter().enumerate() {
        let w = u16::try_from(text::width(line)).unwrap_or(0);
        let x = area.x + area.width.saturating_sub(w) / 2;
        let style = if i == 0 {
            bold(Token::Fg)
        } else {
            on(Token::Fg3, Token::Bg0)
        };
        let mut pen = Pen::new(buf, x, top + u16::try_from(i).unwrap_or(0), area.right(), g);
        pen.text(line, style);
    }
}

File `crates/planrun-tui/src/draw.rs` (full contents):

```rust
//! Painting on the cell grid: a pen that writes styled text left to right on one row, key chips,
//! bands and hairlines. Every view draws through these, so the spacing rules live in one place.

use ratatui::buffer::Buffer;
use ratatui::layout::Rect;
use ratatui::style::{Modifier, Style};

use crate::glyph::{Glyph, Glyphs};
use crate::text;
use crate::theme::{Token, bold, fg, on};

/// Writes styled text along one row, from `x` up to `right` (exclusive); text past `right` is
/// cut with an ellipsis.
pub struct Pen<'b> {
    buf: &'b mut Buffer,
    /// The next column.
    pub x: u16,
    y: u16,
    right: u16,
    glyphs: Glyphs,
}

impl<'b> Pen<'b> {
    /// A pen at (`x`, `y`) that stops before `right`.
    pub fn new(buf: &'b mut Buffer, x: u16, y: u16, right: u16, glyphs: Glyphs) -> Pen<'b> {
        Pen {
            buf,
            x,
            y,
            right,
            glyphs,
        }
    }

    /// The cells left before `right`.
    pub fn room(&self) -> usize {
        usize::from(self.right.saturating_sub(self.x))
    }

    /// Writes `s` in `style`.
    pub fn text(&mut self, s: &str, style: Style) -> &mut Self {
        let room = self.room();
        if room == 0 {
            return self;
        }
        let shown = text::fit(s, room, self.glyphs.get(Glyph::Ellipsis));
        let w = text::width(&shown);
        self.buf.set_stringn(self.x, self.y, &shown, room, style);
        self.x += u16::try_from(w).unwrap_or(u16::MAX);
        self
    }

    /// Writes `glyph` in `style`.
    pub fn glyph(&mut self, glyph: Glyph, style: Style) -> &mut Self {
        let s = self.glyphs.get(glyph);
        self.text(s, style)
    }

    /// Skips `n` cells.
    pub fn gap(&mut self, n: u16) -> &mut Self {
        self.x = (self.x + n).min(self.right);
        self
    }

    /// Moves to column `x` (never backwards).
    pub fn to(&mut self, x: u16) -> &mut Self {
        self.x = self.x.max(x).min(self.right);
        self
    }

    /// Writes `s` so that it ends at `right` (or right after what is already written).
    pub fn right(&mut self, s: &str, style: Style) -> &mut Self {
        let w = u16::try_from(text::width(s)).unwrap_or(u16::MAX);
        let at = self.right.saturating_sub(w).max(self.x);
        self.x = at;
        self.text(s, style)
    }

    /// Writes `s` padded or cut to exactly `cells` cells.
    pub fn cell(&mut self, s: &str, cells: u16, style: Style) -> &mut Self {
        let start = self.x;
        self.text(
            &text::fit(s, usize::from(cells), self.glyphs.get(Glyph::Ellipsis)),
            style,
        );
        self.x = (start + cells).min(self.right);
        self
    }

    /// A key chip: ` key ` in bold on bg2, then `label` in fg2, on the band's background.
    pub fn chip(&mut self, key: &str, label: &str, band: Token) -> &mut Self {
        self.text(&format!(" {key} "), bold(Token::Fg).bg(Token::Bg2.color()));
        self.text(&format!(" {label}"), on(Token::Fg2, band))
    }
}

/// Fills `area` with `style` (spaces).
pub fn fill(buf: &mut Buffer, area: Rect, style: Style) {
    for y in area.top()..area.bottom() {
        for x in area.left()..area.right() {
            if let Some(cell) = buf.cell_mut((x, y)) {
                cell.reset();
                cell.set_style(style);
            }
        }
    }
}

/// A vertical hairline at column `x` from `top` to `bottom` (exclusive).
pub fn vrule(buf: &mut Buffer, x: u16, top: u16, bottom: u16, glyphs: Glyphs) {
    for y in top..bottom {
        buf.set_string(x, y, glyphs.get(Glyph::VRule), on(Token::Rule, Token::Bg0));
    }
}

/// A horizontal hairline on row `y` from `left` to `right` (exclusive).
pub fn hrule(buf: &mut Buffer, left: u16, right: u16, y: u16, glyphs: Glyphs) {
    let line = glyphs
        .get(Glyph::HRule)
        .repeat(usize::from(right.saturating_sub(left)));
    buf.set_string(left, y, line, fg(Token::Rule));
}

/// A section title in a panel: `TITLE ───── right`, the title bold fg3, the rule to `right`.
pub fn section(
    buf: &mut Buffer,
    area: Rect,
    y: u16,
    title: &str,
    tail: &[(String, Style)],
    glyphs: Glyphs,
) {
    let tail_w: usize = tail.iter().map(|(s, _)| text::width(s)).sum();
    let mut pen = Pen::new(buf, area.x, y, area.right(), glyphs);
    pen.text(title, bold(Token::Fg3)).gap(1);
    let rule_end = usize::from(area.right()).saturating_sub(tail_w + usize::from(!tail.is_empty()));
    let n = rule_end.saturating_sub(usize::from(pen.x));
    pen.text(&glyphs.get(Glyph::HRule).repeat(n), fg(Token::Rule));
    if !tail.is_empty() {
        pen.gap(1);
        for (s, style) in tail {
            pen.text(s, *style);
        }
    }
}

/// A panel's band: bg1, with `▎` and the title in act bold when the panel has focus, the title
/// in fg2 bold otherwise, then `detail` in fg3.
pub fn band(
    buf: &mut Buffer,
    area: Rect,
    focused: bool,
    title: &str,
    detail: &str,
    glyphs: Glyphs,
) -> u16 {
    let row = Rect::new(area.x, area.y, area.width, 1);
    fill(buf, row, on(Token::Fg, Token::Bg1));
    let mut pen = Pen::new(buf, area.x, area.y, area.right(), glyphs);
    if focused {
        pen.glyph(Glyph::Focus, on(Token::Act, Token::Bg1)).gap(1);
        pen.text(title, bold(Token::Act).bg(Token::Bg1.color()));
    } else {
        pen.gap(2);
        pen.text(title, bold(Token::Fg2).bg(Token::Bg1.color()));
    }
    pen.gap(2).text(detail, on(Token::Fg3, Token::Bg1));
    pen.x
}

/// The selected row's style: bg2 under the whole row, `▌` in act at its first cell.
pub fn select_row(buf: &mut Buffer, area: Rect, y: u16, glyphs: Glyphs) {
    for x in area.left()..area.right() {
        if let Some(cell) = buf.cell_mut((x, y)) {
            cell.set_bg(Token::Bg2.color());
        }
    }
    buf.set_string(
        area.x,
        y,
        glyphs.get(Glyph::Select),
        on(Token::Act, Token::Bg2),
    );
}

/// `style` made bold.
pub fn strong(style: Style) -> Style {
    style.add_modifier(Modifier::BOLD)
}

File `crates/planrun-tui/src/driver.rs` (full contents):

```rust
//! The loop's core, shared by the terminal and the tests (decision D93): it feeds the app keys,
//! ticks and messages, carries out the effects the app asks for, and draws. The terminal adds
//! crossterm's events; the tests a `TestBackend` and scripted keys.

use std::panic::{AssertUnwindSafe, catch_unwind};
use std::sync::mpsc::{Receiver, Sender, channel};
use std::time::{Duration, Instant};

use ratatui::Terminal;
use ratatui::backend::Backend;

use crate::app::{App, Effect, Msg};
use crate::jobs::panic_message;
use crate::keys::Key;

/// The app, its terminal, and the channel its threads answer on.
pub struct Core<B: Backend> {
    app: App,
    terminal: Terminal<B>,
    tx: Sender<Msg>,
    rx: Receiver<Msg>,
}

impl<B: Backend> Core<B> {
    /// The core over `terminal`.
    pub fn new(app: App, terminal: Terminal<B>) -> Core<B> {
        let (tx, rx) = channel();
        Core {
            app,
            terminal,
            tx,
            rx,
        }
    }

    /// The app.
    pub fn app(&self) -> &App {
        &self.app
    }

    /// The terminal.
    pub fn terminal(&self) -> &Terminal<B> {
        &self.terminal
    }

    /// Whether the app is done.
    pub fn done(&self) -> bool {
        self.app.done()
    }

    /// Starts the app's first jobs.
    pub fn start(&mut self) {
        let effects = self.app.start();
        self.apply(effects);
    }

    /// Feeds a key.
    pub fn key(&mut self, key: Key) {
        let effects = self.app.key(key);
        self.apply(effects);
    }

    /// Lets time pass.
    pub fn tick(&mut self) {
        let effects = self.app.tick();
        self.apply(effects);
    }

    /// Hands the app every message that has arrived; true when there was one.
    pub fn pump(&mut self) -> bool {
        let mut any = false;
        while let Ok(msg) = self.rx.try_recv() {
            any = true;
            let effects = self.app.msg(msg);
            self.apply(effects);
        }
        any
    }

    /// Waits up to `timeout` for `until` to hold, handing the app messages as they arrive.
    pub fn wait(&mut self, timeout: Duration, until: impl Fn(&App) -> bool) -> bool {
        let end = Instant::now() + timeout;
        loop {
            self.pump();
            if until(&self.app) {
                return true;
            }
            let left = end.saturating_duration_since(Instant::now());
            if left.is_zero() {
                return false;
            }
            if let Ok(msg) = self.rx.recv_timeout(left.min(Duration::from_millis(20))) {
                let effects = self.app.msg(msg);
                self.apply(effects);
            }
        }
    }

    /// Draws the app.
    pub fn draw(&mut self) -> Result<(), B::Error> {
        let app = &self.app;
        self.terminal.draw(|frame| app.draw(frame.buffer_mut()))?;
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Forces a full redraw after the screen was left.
    pub fn redraw(&mut self) -> Result<(), B::Error> {
        self.terminal.clear()?;
        self.draw()
    }

    fn apply(&mut self, effects: Vec<Effect>) {
        for effect in effects {
            match effect {
                Effect::Job(job) => {
                    let tx = self.tx.clone();
                    let ctx = self.app.context();
                    let failed = job.on_failure();
                    std::thread::spawn(move || {
                        let done = match catch_unwind(AssertUnwindSafe(|| job.run(&ctx))) {
                            Ok(done) => done,
                            Err(payload) => failed(format!(
                                "the job panicked: {}",
                                panic_message(payload.as_ref())
                            )),
                        };
                        let _ = tx.send(Msg::Done(done));
                    });
                }
                Effect::Quit => self.app.quit = true,
            }
        }
    }
}

//! The glyphs (docs/tui-design.md §5, decision D89): every one is a single cell wide, has an
//! ASCII fallback, and `PLANRUN_ASCII=1` switches the whole set to the fallbacks. No Nerd Font,
//! no wide or emoji glyph. A unit test pins the widths; the crate README holds the manual check
//! in Windows Terminal with Cascadia Mono.

/// One glyph the TUI draws itself (plan text is shown as written).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum Glyph {
    /// `○` pending.
    Pending,
    /// `◉` running.
    Running,
    /// `✓` done, verified.
    Done,
    /// `✕` failed.
    Failed,
    /// `⊘` skipped.
    Skipped,
    /// `◆` waiting for you.
    Waiting,
    /// `!` mismatch, warning.
    Warning,
    /// `·` dry run: not simulated; also the separator dot.
    Dot,
    /// `◌` background work.
    Background,
    /// `◐◓◑◒` the console's live line, frame by frame.
    Spin(u8),
    /// `+` WriteFile.
    Write,
    /// `±` ApplyPatch.
    Patch,
    /// `≈` ReplaceExact.
    Replace,
    /// `−` Delete.
    Delete,
    /// `∗` Generated.
    Generated,
    /// `$` RunScript.
    Script,
    /// `⊙` Commit.
    Commit,
    /// `¶` Note.
    Note,
    /// `⚑` Manual.
    Manual,
    /// `›` between crumbs.
    Crumb,
    /// `│` a vertical hairline.
    VRule,
    /// `─` a horizontal hairline.
    HRule,
    /// `▎` the focused panel's mark.
    Focus,
    /// `▌` the selected row's mark.
    Select,
    /// `▐` the dock's band.
    Band,
    /// `▾` unfolded.
    Open,
    /// `▸` folded.
    Closed,
    /// `⇣` the console follows.
    Follow,
    /// `←` points back.
    Left,
    /// `◂` an aligned output line.
    Aligned,
    /// `→` leads to.
    Right,
    /// `…` text cut short.
    Ellipsis,
    /// `↑` an update.
    Up,
    /// `█` coverage: action.
    CovAction,
    /// `▪` coverage: absorbed.
    CovAbsorbed,
    /// `▒` coverage: manifest.
    CovManifest,
    /// `░` coverage: note.
    CovNote,
    /// `⏎` enter after a typed word.
    Enter,
    /// `▏` the input caret.
    Caret,
}

impl Glyph {
    /// Every glyph, for the width test.
    pub const ALL: [Glyph; 42] = [
        Glyph::Pending,
        Glyph::Running,
        Glyph::Done,
        Glyph::Failed,
        Glyph::Skipped,
        Glyph::Waiting,
        Glyph::Warning,
        Glyph::Dot,
        Glyph::Background,
        Glyph::Spin(0),
        Glyph::Spin(1),
        Glyph::Spin(2),
        Glyph::Spin(3),
        Glyph::Write,
        Glyph::Patch,
        Glyph::Replace,
        Glyph::Delete,
        Glyph::Generated,
        Glyph::Script,
        Glyph::Commit,
        Glyph::Note,
        Glyph::Manual,
        Glyph::Crumb,
        Glyph::VRule,
        Glyph::HRule,
        Glyph::Focus,
        Glyph::Select,
        Glyph::Band,
        Glyph::Open,
        Glyph::Closed,
        Glyph::Follow,
        Glyph::Left,
        Glyph::Aligned,
        Glyph::Right,
        Glyph::Ellipsis,
        Glyph::Up,
        Glyph::CovAction,
        Glyph::CovAbsorbed,
        Glyph::CovManifest,
        Glyph::CovNote,
        Glyph::Enter,
        Glyph::Caret,
    ];

    /// The glyph.
    pub fn unicode(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Glyph::Pending => "○",
            Glyph::Running => "◉",
            Glyph::Done => "✓",
            Glyph::Failed => "✕",
            Glyph::Skipped => "⊘",
            Glyph::Waiting => "◆",
            Glyph::Warning => "!",
            Glyph::Dot => "·",
            Glyph::Background => "◌",
            Glyph::Spin(n) => ["◐", "◓", "◑", "◒"][usize::from(n % 4)],
            Glyph::Write => "+",
            Glyph::Patch => "±",
            Glyph::Replace => "≈",
            Glyph::Delete => "−",
            Glyph::Generated => "∗",
            Glyph::Script => "$",
            Glyph::Commit => "⊙",
            Glyph::Note => "¶",
            Glyph::Manual => "⚑",
            Glyph::Crumb => "›",
            Glyph::VRule => "│",
            Glyph::HRule => "─",
            Glyph::Focus => "▎",
            Glyph::Select => "▌",
            Glyph::Band => "▐",
            Glyph::Open => "▾",
            Glyph::Closed => "▸",
            Glyph::Follow => "⇣",
            Glyph::Left => "←",
            Glyph::Aligned => "◂",
            Glyph::Right => "→",
            Glyph::Ellipsis => "…",
            Glyph::Up => "↑",
            Glyph::CovAction => "█",
            Glyph::CovAbsorbed => "▪",
            Glyph::CovManifest => "▒",
            Glyph::CovNote => "░",
            Glyph::Enter => "⏎",
            Glyph::Caret => "▏",
        }
    }

    /// Its ASCII fallback.
    pub fn ascii(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Glyph::Pending => "o",
            Glyph::Running => "*",
            Glyph::Done => "v",
            Glyph::Failed => "x",
            Glyph::Skipped | Glyph::Delete | Glyph::HRule => "-",
            Glyph::Waiting => "?",
            Glyph::Warning => "!",
            Glyph::Dot | Glyph::Note | Glyph::CovNote => ".",
            Glyph::Background | Glyph::Patch => "~",
            Glyph::Spin(n) => ["-", "\\", "|", "/"][usize::from(n % 4)],
            Glyph::Write => "+",
            Glyph::Replace | Glyph::CovAbsorbed => "=",
            Glyph::Generated => "*",
            Glyph::Script => "$",
            Glyph::Commit => "@",
            Glyph::Manual => "M",
            Glyph::Crumb | Glyph::Right | Glyph::Select | Glyph::Closed => ">",
            Glyph::VRule | Glyph::Focus | Glyph::Band | Glyph::Caret => "|",
            Glyph::Open | Glyph::Follow => "v",
            Glyph::Left | Glyph::Aligned | Glyph::Enter => "<",
            Glyph::Ellipsis => "~",
            Glyph::Up => "^",
            Glyph::CovAction => "#",
            Glyph::CovManifest => ":",
        }
    }
}

/// The glyph set in use: Unicode, or every fallback.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Default)]
pub struct Glyphs {
    ascii: bool,
}

impl Glyphs {
    /// The Unicode set.
    pub fn unicode() -> Glyphs {
        Glyphs { ascii: false }
    }

    /// The ASCII fallbacks.
    pub fn ascii() -> Glyphs {
        Glyphs { ascii: true }
    }

    /// `PLANRUN_ASCII=1` selects the fallbacks.
    pub fn from_env() -> Glyphs {
        Glyphs {
            ascii: std::env::var_os("PLANRUN_ASCII").is_some_and(|v| v == "1"),
        }
    }

    /// The text of `glyph`.
    pub fn get(self, glyph: Glyph) -> &'static str {
        if self.ascii {
            glyph.ascii()
        } else {
            glyph.unicode()
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use unicode_width::UnicodeWidthStr;

    use super::*;

    #[test]
    fn every_glyph_and_fallback_is_one_cell_wide() {
        for glyph in Glyph::ALL {
            assert_eq!(glyph.unicode().width(), 1, "{glyph:?} {}", glyph.unicode());
            assert_eq!(glyph.unicode().chars().count(), 1, "{glyph:?}");
            assert_eq!(glyph.ascii().len(), 1, "{glyph:?}");
            assert!(glyph.ascii().is_ascii(), "{glyph:?}");
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn the_switch_selects_the_fallbacks() {
        assert_eq!(Glyphs::unicode().get(Glyph::Manual), "⚑");
        assert_eq!(Glyphs::ascii().get(Glyph::Manual), "M");
        assert_eq!(Glyphs::ascii().get(Glyph::Spin(5)), "\\");
    }
}
